It's really exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of these looping thoughts or tension?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually. I have been avoiding things. It's getting pretty bad at work, honestly. I've started falling behind on my support tickets because I just keep double-checking the most basic things, like if I reset the right user's password or if I logged the ticket correctly. It takes me twice as long to do anything, and my coworkers have had to pick up the slack. Which makes me feel terrible, because my coworker Dave just had a baby. Well, his wife did, obviously. It's a little girl, they named her Harper. They brought her into the office last week and she was wearing this tiny little yellow cardigan with duck buttons. It was so cute. I wanted to knit her a blanket—I tried to get into knitting during the pandemic like everyone else. I bought all this chunky merino wool yarn in this really pretty sage green color, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were always way too tight, kind of like how my shoulders feel now, I guess. \n\nBut um, yeah, because of all the checking and the looping thoughts, I'm just so overwhelmed by the end of the day. I've actually skipped salsa night the last few weeks. And I mentor this high school girl on weekends for a Women in Tech program, but I've canceled on her twice recently because I just... I couldn't face it. I felt too wound up to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding these situations because you're afraid something bad will happen if you don't check everything a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not exactly. It's not like I think something terrible is going to happen, like the building will burn down or someone will die if I don't check. It's more just... I can't stop feeling like I missed something, and if I don't check, I'll just feel wrong. It's hard to explain. \n\nIt kind of reminds me of when I was helping my dad organize his garage last summer. He has this massive collection of vintage license plates, like hundreds of them from all over the country. He started collecting them when he went on this cross-country road trip in his twenties in a Volkswagen bus. Those buses are so iconic, but they break down all the time. He told me he had to replace the alternator somewhere in New Mexico and the guy at the auto shop traded him a 1972 Arizona plate for a six-pack of beer. Which is a pretty good trade, honestly, considering how much those vintage plates go for on eBay now. I actually tried selling some of my old college textbooks on eBay once, but the shipping costs were ridiculous. By the time I paid for the bubble mailers and the postage, I think I made like three dollars. I ended up just donating the rest of them to the library... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, being afraid something bad will happen. No, it's not a specific fear like that. It's just this nagging feeling that I have to check, or I won't be able to stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're performing these checks out of a sense of compulsion, rather than just being careful?\n\nPatient: Um, compulsion... I mean, I guess it feels like I just *have* to do it, you know? Like I can't just move on. It's not like I'm just being thorough, because I know I already checked it. It's more like my brain won't let it go. It's kind of like when you get a song stuck in your head and you have to listen to it all the way through to get it out. \n\nSpeaking of songs, I had that one song by Fleetwood Mac stuck in my head all morning. \"Dreams,\" I think it's called. My mom used to play their *Rumours* album on vinyl all the time when I was a kid. Vinyl is making such a huge comeback, isn't it? I saw this whole section at Target the other day, right next to the electronics. It's funny because when I started working in IT support, everything was moving to the cloud, and now people are wanting these physical, tangible things again. Like my friend Sarah, she just bought a typewriter. A literal, heavy metal typewriter from an antique mall. She says it helps her write her poetry without getting distracted by the internet. I tried writing poetry in high school, but everything I wrote sounded like bad song lyrics. I think I rhymed \"tears\" with \"fears\" in every single poem. \n\nBut um, yeah. It definitely feels more like a compulsion than just being careful. It's just really hard to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these compulsions to check things are interfering with your daily life or causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... it definitely is causing distress. Like I said, I'm falling behind at work, and I'm just so exhausted by the time I log off. It's really getting in the way. It's like I don't have energy for anything else, which is why I've been skipping things like salsa and mentoring. \n\nIt actually reminds me of when I tried to do this 30-day yoga challenge on YouTube last year. I thought it would help me relax, you know? The instructor was this really calm woman who always filmed in these beautiful, sunlit rooms with tons of plants. I love house plants, but I can never keep them alive. I bought a fiddle leaf fig once because they look so nice in interior design magazines, but it dropped all its leaves within a month. I read somewhere that they're super sensitive to drafts, and my apartment has these really old, drafty windows. The landlord keeps saying he's going to replace them, but he's been saying that since I moved in three years ago. He's this older guy who always wears these bright Hawaiian shirts, even in the middle of winter. It's kind of endearing, but also, like, I just want my windows fixed, you know? The heating bill gets so high in January. \n\nBut um... what were we talking about? Oh, distress. Yeah. It's just really frustrating because I know I don't need to check these things, but I do it anyway, and it just eats up my whole day. It's really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, all the time. It's like my brain just gets stuck on a loop. Even when I tell myself I've already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, the thoughts keep looping, and one 'what if' turns into five more before I can pull myself back. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're trying to watch a video online and it just keeps buffering, you know? Just spinning and spinning. I had to deal with a lot of buffering issues when I was setting up my mom's smart TV last weekend. She bought this huge 65-inch screen because she wanted to watch all those British baking shows in high definition. I don't really get the appeal of baking shows, honestly. Like, you can't even smell or taste what they're making, so what's the point? I tried baking sourdough bread during the pandemic like everyone else, but my starter just smelled like dirty socks and never bubbled right. I think I used the wrong kind of flour. There are so many kinds of flour at the grocery store now—almond flour, coconut flour, oat flour. It's overwhelming. I usually just end up buying the regular all-purpose stuff in the yellow bag. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the looping thoughts. It's exactly like that. I try to think about something else, but the 'what ifs' just keep multiplying and I can't shut them off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel compelled to perform specific physical actions, like touching or tapping, as part of your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Um, touching or tapping? No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I have to tap a desk a certain number of times or anything like that. It's mostly just the checking things on my computer or double-checking the locks. \n\nSpeaking of locks, I actually had to call a locksmith last month because I locked myself out of my car at the grocery store. It was so embarrassing. I had just bought all these groceries, including a pint of ice cream—mint chocolate chip, which is my absolute favorite, though my boyfriend says it tastes like toothpaste. I don't get that at all, it's so refreshing! But anyway, I was standing there in the parking lot for like an hour waiting for the guy to show up, and the ice cream totally melted. The locksmith was this really young guy, maybe early twenties, and he opened my car in like two seconds with this weird inflatable wedge thing. It made me realize how easy it is to break into a car, which honestly just gave me something else to worry about. I started looking up car alarm systems when I got home, but they're so expensive, and my car is like ten years old anyway. It's a Honda Civic, which are supposed to run forever, right? But the AC has been making this weird rattling noise lately... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, tapping. No, I don't really do anything like that. Just the checking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, unwanted, and recur over and over?\n\nPatient: Um, intrusive thoughts? I mean, I guess it depends on what you mean by unwanted. Like, I don't really have thoughts that just pop in out of nowhere that are, like, weird or disturbing, if that's what you mean. It's really just the worrying about work and making mistakes, like I mentioned before. \n\nBut talking about unwanted things popping up, my computer at work has been getting all these weird pop-up notifications lately. It's so annoying. I'm a support specialist, so I should know how to fix it, right? But our IT department installed this new security software last week, and it's totally overzealous. It blocks everything. I couldn't even access this forum I use to troubleshoot printer issues. Printers are honestly the worst part of my job. They always jam, or they run out of cyan ink when you're just trying to print a black-and-white document. Why do they even need cyan to print black text? It feels like a total scam by the ink companies. I watched this whole documentary on streaming about planned obsolescence and how companies design things to break so you have to buy more. It was super depressing. It made me want to go live off the grid in a cabin somewhere, but I'm terrified of bugs. Spiders especially. I saw this huge wolf spider in my bathroom last summer and I literally couldn't go in there for two days. I had to shower at my boyfriend's place. His apartment is so clean, but he uses this really strong pine-scented cleaner that gives me a headache if I'm there too long...\n\nBut um, what was the question? Intrusive thoughts? Yeah, no, I don't really have anything like that. Just the regular worrying about the spreadsheets and emails.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel more like something that's happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: Um, I think... It's just me wanting to be sure. \n\nWhich is kind of like how my boyfriend is with his morning routine. He has to have his coffee exactly at 7:00 AM, and it has to be this specific dark roast from a local roaster. If he runs out and has to drink the stuff I buy from the grocery store—the kind in the big plastic tub—he gets so grumpy. I don't really get it, coffee is just coffee to me. I usually just put so much oat milk and vanilla syrup in mine that it basically tastes like a milkshake anyway. I've been trying to cut back on the sugar, though, because my dentist told me I have the start of a cavity on one of my back molars. I hate going to the dentist. The scraping noise they make on your teeth just goes right through me, it's like nails on a chalkboard. I actually had to ask them to let me wear my noise-canceling headphones last time I went for a cleaning. They're these really nice Sony ones my brother got me for my birthday. He works at Best Buy, so he gets a pretty good discount on electronics, which is nice when you need a new phone or whatever. \n\nBut um, yeah, the checking. It's definitely just me doing it. It doesn't feel like it's against my will, it's just... I just have to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Are these checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um, affecting my relationships? I don't... I don't think so. That bootcamp was so intense, the instructor was this guy who used to work at Google and he talked incredibly fast, like he'd had eight shots of espresso... \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Friends? Yeah, no, my checking things doesn't really bother anyone. I don't think they even notice, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to constantly check things is significantly interfering with your ability to enjoy your daily activities or maintain your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, interfering with things? It's gotten to the point where my coworkers are having to pick up my slack, which makes me feel awful. \n\nWhich is exactly why I hate group projects. Even back in grad school, there was always that one person who didn't do anything, and you'd have to scramble at the last minute to fix their part of the presentation. We had this one project on database architecture, and my partner literally copied and pasted an entire Wikipedia article for his section. I had to stay up until 4:00 AM rewriting it so we wouldn't get expelled for plagiarism. I drank so much Red Bull that night my heart was palpitating for two days. I don't touch energy drinks anymore. Actually, I saw this article the other day about how energy drinks are causing a spike in heart issues for people in their twenties. It's crazy what they put in those things. Taurine, guarana... I don't even know what guarana is. It sounds like some kind of tropical lizard, doesn't it? Like an iguana, but... guarana. I used to want a pet lizard when I was a kid, a bearded dragon specifically, but my mom said they smell bad and you have to feed them live crickets. I can't do live bugs. Like I said before with the spiders, I just... I can't. \n\nBut um, yeah, so work has been tough. And outside of work, I've actually skipped my Thursday dance nights the last few weeks. Um, to prevent something bad? Like someone getting hurt? No, I don't... I don't think it's like that. I'm not worried about, you know, a burglar breaking in or my apartment catching on fire or getting some terrible disease. It's not about danger. It's more just... I have to know I did it right. I just have this nagging feeling that I messed up a step or forgot something small. 

Which is so frustrating because I'm usually so detail-oriented. Like when I was setting up my new mechanical keyboard last week—I finally splurged on the Keychron Q1, the one with the aluminum body and the Gateron Phantom Red switches. It sounds like absolute butter when you type. Anyway, I spent like three hours just lubing the stabilizers because if there's even a tiny bit of rattle on the spacebar, it drives me completely up the wall. My boyfriend thinks I'm insane for spending that much time on a keyboard, but he plays golf, which is literally just hitting a little white ball into a hole for four hours, so I don't think he has room to judge. Plus, golf courses are terrible for the environment. All that water they use just to keep the grass perfectly green in the middle of summer? It's so wasteful. I watched this whole documentary about water rights in the Southwest, and it's actually terrifying how low the reservoirs are getting. Lake Mead is basically just a puddle at this point. I tried to bring it up at my family's Thanksgiving dinner, but my uncle Dave—who works in commercial real estate—started getting all defensive about zoning laws, and it turned into this huge argument over the sweet potato casserole. I didn't even get to eat any of the casserole, which is a tragedy because my mom puts those little toasted marshmallows on top...

Sorry, I'm rambling again, aren't I? But yeah, to answer your question, no, I'm not checking things to stop something terrible from happening. It's just my own worry about making a mistake.

It just feels like it's always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same things or feeling like you need to do certain things over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I don't really have to *do* things over and over, like turning a light switch or anything like that. And then one \"what if\" turns into five more before I can pull myself back. \n\nIt's actually why I started drinking chamomile tea instead of coffee in the afternoons. The owner is this older guy who always wears these really elaborate bow ties. He told me he used to be a jazz musician in the 70s, playing the saxophone, which is such a cool instrument. It was so embarrassing, especially because my crush was sitting right in the front row...\n\nWait, sorry. But yeah, the thoughts just loop and loop. It's really exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of these looping thoughts or tension?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually. I have been avoiding things. It's getting pretty bad at work, honestly. I've started falling behind on my support tickets because I just keep double-checking the most basic things, like if I reset the right user's password or if I logged the ticket correctly. It takes me twice as long to do anything, and my coworkers have had to pick up the slack. Which makes me feel terrible, because my coworker Dave just had a baby. Well, his wife did, obviously. I couldn't face it. I felt too wound up to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding these situations because you're afraid something bad will happen if you don't check everything a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not exactly. It's just this nagging feeling that I have to check, or I won't be able to stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're performing these checks out of a sense of compulsion, rather than just being careful?\n\nPatient: Um, compulsion... It's really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, all the time. Like, you can't even smell or taste what they're making, so what's the point? Even when I tell myself I've already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, the thoughts keep looping, and one 'what if' turns into five more before I can pull myself back. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're trying to watch a video online and it just keeps buffering, you know? Just spinning and spinning. I had to deal with a lot of buffering issues when I was setting up my mom's smart TV last weekend. She bought this huge 65-inch screen because she wanted to watch all those British baking shows in high definition. I don't really get the appeal of baking shows, honestly. Like, you can't even smell or taste what they're making, so what's the point? I tried baking sourdough bread during the pandemic like everyone else, but my starter just smelled like dirty socks and never bubbled right. I think I used the wrong kind of flour. There are so many kinds of flour at the grocery store now—almond flour, coconut flour, oat flour. It's overwhelming. I usually just end up buying the regular all-purpose stuff in the yellow bag. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the looping thoughts. It's exactly like that. I try to think about something else, but the 'what ifs' just keep multiplying and I can't shut them off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel compelled to perform specific physical actions, like touching or tapping, as part of your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Um, touching or tapping? No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I have to tap a desk a certain number of times or anything like that. It's mostly just the checking things on my computer or double-checking the locks. \n\nSpeaking of locks, I actually had to call a locksmith last month because I locked myself out of my car at the grocery store. It was so embarrassing. I had just bought all these groceries, including a pint of ice cream—mint chocolate chip, which is my absolute favorite, though my boyfriend says it tastes like toothpaste. I don't get that at all, it's so refreshing! But anyway, I was standing there in the parking lot for like an hour waiting for the guy to show up, and the ice cream totally melted. The locksmith was this really young guy, maybe early twenties, and he opened my car in like two seconds with this weird inflatable wedge thing. It made me realize how easy it is to break into a car, which honestly just gave me something else to worry about. I started looking up car alarm systems when I got home, but they're so expensive, and my car is like ten years old anyway. It's a Honda Civic, which are supposed to run forever, right? But the AC has been making this weird rattling noise lately... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, tapping. No, I don't really do anything like that. Just the checking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, unwanted, and recur over and over?\n\nPatient: Um, intrusive thoughts? I mean, I guess it depends on what you mean by unwanted. Like, I don't really have thoughts that just pop in out of nowhere that are, like, weird or disturbing, if that's what you mean. It's really just the worrying about work and making mistakes, like I mentioned before. \n\nBut talking about unwanted things popping up, my computer at work has been getting all these weird pop-up notifications lately. It's so annoying. I'm a support specialist, so I should know how to fix it, right? But our IT department installed this new security software last week, and it's totally overzealous. It blocks everything. I couldn't even access this forum I use to troubleshoot printer issues. Printers are honestly the worst part of my job. They always jam, or they run out of cyan ink when you're just trying to print a black-and-white document. Why do they even need cyan to print black text? It feels like a total scam by the ink companies. I watched this whole documentary on streaming about planned obsolescence and how companies design things to break so you have to buy more. It was super depressing. It made me want to go live off the grid in a cabin somewhere, but I'm terrified of bugs. Spiders especially. I saw this huge wolf spider in my bathroom last summer and I literally couldn't go in there for two days. I had to shower at my boyfriend's place. His apartment is so clean, but he uses this really strong pine-scented cleaner that gives me a headache if I'm there too long...\n\nBut um, what was the question? Intrusive thoughts? Yeah, no, I don't really have anything like that. Just the regular worrying about the spreadsheets and emails.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel more like something that's happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: Um, I think... I guess they feel like part of me, because it's just my brain trying to make sure I didn't mess up. I don't feel like some outside force is making me check my emails or the locks. It's just me wanting to be sure. \n\nWhich is kind of like how my boyfriend is with his morning routine. He has to have his coffee exactly at 7:00 AM, and it has to be this specific dark roast from a local roaster. If he runs out and has to drink the stuff I buy from the grocery store—the kind in the big plastic tub—he gets so grumpy. I don't really get it, coffee is just coffee to me. I usually just put so much oat milk and vanilla syrup in mine that it basically tastes like a milkshake anyway. I've been trying to cut back on the sugar, though, because my dentist told me I have the start of a cavity on one of my back molars. I hate going to the dentist. The scraping noise they make on your teeth just goes right through me, it's like nails on a chalkboard. I actually had to ask them to let me wear my noise-canceling headphones last time I went for a cleaning. They're these really nice Sony ones my brother got me for my birthday. He works at Best Buy, so he gets a pretty good discount on electronics, which is nice when you need a new phone or whatever. \n\nBut um, yeah, the checking. It's definitely just me doing it. It doesn't feel like it's against my will, it's just... I just have to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Are these checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um, affecting my relationships? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, my boyfriend knows I double-check the locks and stuff, but he doesn't really care. And my friends haven't said anything about it. So, no, not really. \n\nSpeaking of my friends, though, my best friend Sarah is getting married next spring, and she just asked me to be a bridesmaid. Which is super exciting, obviously, but she wants us all to wear these sage green dresses. And I'm happy for her, it's her big day, but sage green totally washes me out. I have this really pale skin with cool undertones, so I usually stick to jewel tones, like emerald or sapphire? I tried explaining this to her, but she's dead set on this rustic woodland theme. She even wants the reception in this actual barn. Like, an actual working barn that they just clean out for the weekend. I don't know how I feel about eating dinner where horses usually sleep, you know? Plus, I have terrible seasonal allergies. Last time I went to a farm, my eyes swelled up so bad I looked like I'd gone a few rounds in a boxing ring. I had to take Benadryl, which always knocks me out completely. I once slept for fourteen hours straight after taking one, and I missed a whole day of this coding bootcamp I was doing right after grad school. That bootcamp was so intense, the instructor was this guy who used to work at Google and he talked incredibly fast, like he'd had eight shots of espresso... \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Friends? Yeah, no, my checking things doesn't really bother anyone. I don't think they even notice, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to constantly check things is significantly interfering with your ability to enjoy your daily activities or maintain your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, interfering with things? Well, yeah, I guess it is starting to get in the way at work. Like, I've been falling behind on my support tickets lately because I'm just double-checking every single step of these really simple tasks. It's gotten to the point where my coworkers are having to pick up my slack, which makes me feel awful. \n\nWhich is exactly why I hate group projects. Even back in grad school, there was always that one person who didn't do anything, and you'd have to scramble at the last minute to fix their part of the presentation. We had this one project on database architecture, and my partner literally copied and pasted an entire Wikipedia article for his section. I had to stay up until 4:00 AM rewriting it so we wouldn't get expelled for plagiarism. I drank so much Red Bull that night my heart was palpitating for two days. I don't touch energy drinks anymore. Actually, I saw this article the other day about how energy drinks are causing a spike in heart issues for people in their twenties. It's crazy what they put in those things. Taurine, guarana... I don't even know what guarana is. It sounds like some kind of tropical lizard, doesn't it? Like an iguana, but... guarana. I used to want a pet lizard when I was a kid, a bearded dragon specifically, but my mom said they smell bad and you have to feed them live crickets. I can't do live bugs. Like I said before with the spiders, I just... I can't. \n\nBut um, yeah, so work has been tough. And outside of work, I've actually skipped my Thursday dance nights the last few weeks. And I mentor this high school robotics team, but I've missed several of those sessions too. I just feel so overwhelmed by the time I get home that I can't bring myself to go out again. So I guess it is interfering, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are repetitive, unwanted, and disturbing to you?\n\nPatient: Um, repetitive and disturbing? No, I don't think so. I don't get, like, scary or weird thoughts popping into my head. It's really just me worrying about making a mistake or forgetting something. \n\nUnless you count getting a really annoying song stuck in your head, which happens to me all the time. Like on Tuesday, the elevator in my apartment building was playing this weird elevator-music version of \"Toxic\" by Britney Spears, and it was just on a loop in my brain for three days. My building's elevator is so incredibly slow, too. It's one of those old ones with the heavy metal doors that feel like they're going to crush you if you don't step through fast enough. My neighbor on the fourth floor—she has this adorable golden retriever named Waffles—she actually got her grocery bag caught in the door once. Waffles was totally fine, he just sat there wagging his tail, but her eggs got smashed everywhere. It took the building superintendent like two whole days to clean it up properly, and the hallway smelled like sulfur the entire time. I tried to submit a complaint to management about it, but they use this awful ticketing system for maintenance requests that looks like it was coded in 1998. Yeah, no, nothing like that. I just have this nagging feeling that I messed up a step or forgot something small. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I'm usually so detail-oriented. I watched this whole documentary about water rights in the Southwest, and it's actually terrifying how low the reservoirs are getting. Lake Mead is basically just a puddle at this point. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, specific mistake versus just a feeling... I guess it's both? But it's not just the checking anymore. It's like... my worry used to just be about the condo and whether I left the stove on or locked the door, but now it doesn't stay on the condo anymore. It just jumps around to everything. Um, emotional pain or distress? I mean... yeah, definitely. It's exhausting. It's just this heavy, awful feeling all the time, like I can't escape my own head. It makes me feel so tense and miserable, just constantly looping over the same worries and checking things. 

Which is exactly why I tried to get back into knitting last weekend, to give my hands and brain something else to do. I went to this amazing little yarn shop downtown—it's called The Tangled Skein, and it smells like lavender and old wood. I bought this gorgeous, hand-dyed merino wool in this deep, moody teal color. I was going to make a scarf, but then I started worrying that my tension was too tight, so I unraveled the first three rows, like, five times. My cat, Barnaby, thought the yarn was a toy, of course. He's a tuxedo cat, and he has this one white paw that looks like he dipped it in paint. He kept batting at the yarn every time I tried to cast on, and eventually, he just sat right on top of the pattern book. Cats are so funny like that, they always know exactly where you need to look and they just plant themselves right there. Like when I'm trying to read on my Kindle, he'll just shove his face right against the screen. I actually had to buy a special heavy-duty case for it because he knocked it off the nightstand once and chipped the corner. 

But yeah, anyway... the checking and the thoughts. It's really upsetting. It just feels like I'm trapped in this cycle of worrying and fixing and worrying again, and it's just... it's really hard to deal with.

Sunday is supposed to be my meal prep day, so that threw off my whole week. I usually make these overnight oats with chia seeds and oat milk. They're supposed to be super good for your digestion, and oat milk is way better for the environment than almond milk, which takes like a gallon of water just to grow one almond, which is crazy when you think about the droughts in California... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, a specific time. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed one. It just feels like it's always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same things or feeling like you need to do certain things over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I don't really have to *do* things over and over, like turning a light switch or anything like that. I have been avoiding things. It's getting pretty bad at work, honestly. I've started falling behind on my support tickets because I just keep double-checking the most basic things, like if I reset the right user's password or if I logged the ticket correctly. It takes me twice as long to do anything, and my coworkers have had to pick up the slack. Which makes me feel terrible, because my coworker Dave just had a baby. Well, his wife did, obviously. It's a little girl, they named her Harper. They brought her into the office last week and she was wearing this tiny little yellow cardigan with duck buttons. It was so cute. I wanted to knit her a blanket—I tried to get into knitting during the pandemic like everyone else. I couldn't face it. I felt too wound up to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding these situations because you're afraid something bad will happen if you don't check everything a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not exactly. It's not like I think something terrible is going to happen, like the building will burn down or someone will die if I don't check. It's more just... I can't stop feeling like I missed something, and if I don't check, I'll just feel wrong. It's hard to explain. \n\nIt kind of reminds me of when I was helping my dad organize his garage last summer. He has this massive collection of vintage license plates, like hundreds of them from all over the country. He started collecting them when he went on this cross-country road trip in his twenties in a Volkswagen bus. Those buses are so iconic, but they break down all the time. He told me he had to replace the alternator somewhere in New Mexico and the guy at the auto shop traded him a 1972 Arizona plate for a six-pack of beer. Which is a pretty good trade, honestly, considering how much those vintage plates go for on eBay now. It's kind of like when you get a song stuck in your head and you have to listen to it all the way through to get it out. \n\nSpeaking of songs, I had that one song by Fleetwood Mac stuck in my head all morning. \"Dreams,\" I think it's called. My mom used to play their *Rumours* album on vinyl all the time when I was a kid. Vinyl is making such a huge comeback, isn't it? I saw this whole section at Target the other day, right next to the electronics. It's funny because when I started working in IT support, everything was moving to the cloud, and now people are wanting these physical, tangible things again. Like my friend Sarah, she just bought a typewriter. A literal, heavy metal typewriter from an antique mall. She says it helps her write her poetry without getting distracted by the internet. I tried writing poetry in high school, but everything I wrote sounded like bad song lyrics. I think I rhymed \"tears\" with \"fears\" in every single poem. \n\nBut um, yeah. It definitely feels more like a compulsion than just being careful. It's just really hard to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these compulsions to check things are interfering with your daily life or causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... it definitely is causing distress. Like I said, I'm falling behind at work, and I'm just so exhausted by the time I log off. It's really getting in the way. It's like I don't have energy for anything else, which is why I've been skipping things like salsa and mentoring. \n\nIt actually reminds me of when I tried to do this 30-day yoga challenge on YouTube last year. I thought it would help me relax, you know? The instructor was this really calm woman who always filmed in these beautiful, sunlit rooms with tons of plants. I love house plants, but I can never keep them alive. I bought a fiddle leaf fig once because they look so nice in interior design magazines, but it dropped all its leaves within a month. I read somewhere that they're super sensitive to drafts, and my apartment has these really old, drafty windows. The landlord keeps saying he's going to replace them, but he's been saying that since I moved in three years ago. He's this older guy who always wears these bright Hawaiian shirts, even in the middle of winter. It's kind of endearing, but also, like, I just want my windows fixed, you know? The heating bill gets so high in January. \n\nBut um... what were we talking about? Oh, distress. Yeah. It's just really frustrating because I know I don't need to check these things, but I do it anyway, and it just eats up my whole day. It's really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, all the time. It's like my brain just gets stuck on a loop. Even when I tell myself I've already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, the thoughts keep looping, and one 'what if' turns into five more before I can pull myself back. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're trying to watch a video online and it just keeps buffering, you know? Just spinning and spinning. I had to deal with a lot of buffering issues when I was setting up my mom's smart TV last weekend. She bought this huge 65-inch screen because she wanted to watch all those British baking shows in high definition. I don't really get the appeal of baking shows, honestly. Like, you can't even smell or taste what they're making, so what's the point? I tried baking sourdough bread during the pandemic like everyone else, but my starter just smelled like dirty socks and never bubbled right. I think I used the wrong kind of flour. There are so many kinds of flour at the grocery store now—almond flour, coconut flour, oat flour. It's overwhelming. I usually just end up buying the regular all-purpose stuff in the yellow bag. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the looping thoughts. It's exactly like that. I try to think about something else, but the 'what ifs' just keep multiplying and I can't shut them off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel compelled to perform specific physical actions, like touching or tapping, as part of your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Um, touching or tapping? No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I have to tap a desk a certain number of times or anything like that. It's mostly just the checking things on my computer or double-checking the locks. \n\nSpeaking of locks, I actually had to call a locksmith last month because I locked myself out of my car at the grocery store. It was so embarrassing. I had just bought all these groceries, including a pint of ice cream—mint chocolate chip, which is my absolute favorite, though my boyfriend says it tastes like toothpaste. I don't get that at all, it's so refreshing! But anyway, I was standing there in the parking lot for like an hour waiting for the guy to show up, and the ice cream totally melted. The locksmith was this really young guy, maybe early twenties, and he opened my car in like two seconds with this weird inflatable wedge thing. It made me realize how easy it is to break into a car, which honestly just gave me something else to worry about. I started looking up car alarm systems when I got home, but they're so expensive, and my car is like ten years old anyway. It's a Honda Civic, which are supposed to run forever, right? But the AC has been making this weird rattling noise lately... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, tapping. No, I don't really do anything like that. Just the checking.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, unwanted, and recur over and over?\n\nPatient: Um, intrusive thoughts? I mean, I guess it depends on what you mean by unwanted. Like, I don't really have thoughts that just pop in out of nowhere that are, like, weird or disturbing, if that's what you mean. It's really just the worrying about work and making mistakes, like I mentioned before. \n\nBut talking about unwanted things popping up, my computer at work has been getting all these weird pop-up notifications lately. It's so annoying. I'm a support specialist, so I should know how to fix it, right? But our IT department installed this new security software last week, and it's totally overzealous. It blocks everything. I couldn't even access this forum I use to troubleshoot printer issues. Printers are honestly the worst part of my job. They always jam, or they run out of cyan ink when you're just trying to print a black-and-white document. Why do they even need cyan to print black text? It feels like a total scam by the ink companies. I watched this whole documentary on streaming about planned obsolescence and how companies design things to break so you have to buy more. It was super depressing. It made me want to go live off the grid in a cabin somewhere, but I'm terrified of bugs. Spiders especially. I saw this huge wolf spider in my bathroom last summer and I literally couldn't go in there for two days. I had to shower at my boyfriend's place. His apartment is so clean, but he uses this really strong pine-scented cleaner that gives me a headache if I'm there too long...\n\nBut um, what was the question? Intrusive thoughts? Yeah, no, I don't really have anything like that. Just the regular worrying about the spreadsheets and emails.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel more like something that's happening to you against your will?\n\nPatient: Um, I think... I guess they feel like part of me, because it's just my brain trying to make sure I didn't mess up. I don't feel like some outside force is making me check my emails or the locks. It's just me wanting to be sure. \n\nWhich is kind of like how my boyfriend is with his morning routine. He has to have his coffee exactly at 7:00 AM, and it has to be this specific dark roast from a local roaster. If he runs out and has to drink the stuff I buy from the grocery store—the kind in the big plastic tub—he gets so grumpy. I don't really get it, coffee is just coffee to me. I usually just put so much oat milk and vanilla syrup in mine that it basically tastes like a milkshake anyway. I've been trying to cut back on the sugar, though, because my dentist told me I have the start of a cavity on one of my back molars. I hate going to the dentist. The scraping noise they make on your teeth just goes right through me, it's like nails on a chalkboard. I actually had to ask them to let me wear my noise-canceling headphones last time I went for a cleaning. They're these really nice Sony ones my brother got me for my birthday. He works at Best Buy, so he gets a pretty good discount on electronics, which is nice when you need a new phone or whatever. \n\nBut um, yeah, the checking. It's definitely just me doing it. It doesn't feel like it's against my will, it's just... I just have to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Are these checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um, affecting my relationships? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, my boyfriend knows I double-check the locks and stuff, but he doesn't really care. And my friends haven't said anything about it. So, no, not really. \n\nSpeaking of my friends, though, my best friend Sarah is getting married next spring, and she just asked me to be a bridesmaid. Which is super exciting, obviously, but she wants us all to wear these sage green dresses. And I'm happy for her, it's her big day, but sage green totally washes me out. I have this really pale skin with cool undertones, so I usually stick to jewel tones, like emerald or sapphire? I tried explaining this to her, but she's dead set on this rustic woodland theme. She even wants the reception in this actual barn. Like, an actual working barn that they just clean out for the weekend. I don't know how I feel about eating dinner where horses usually sleep, you know? Plus, I have terrible seasonal allergies. Last time I went to a farm, my eyes swelled up so bad I looked like I'd gone a few rounds in a boxing ring. I had to take Benadryl, which always knocks me out completely. I once slept for fourteen hours straight after taking one, and I missed a whole day of this coding bootcamp I was doing right after grad school. That bootcamp was so intense, the instructor was this guy who used to work at Google and he talked incredibly fast, like he'd had eight shots of espresso... \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Friends? Yeah, no, my checking things doesn't really bother anyone. I don't think they even notice, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to constantly check things is significantly interfering with your ability to enjoy your daily activities or maintain your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, interfering with things? Well, yeah, I guess it is starting to get in the way at work. Like, I've been falling behind on my support tickets lately because I'm just double-checking every single step of these really simple tasks. It's gotten to the point where my coworkers are having to pick up my slack, which makes me feel awful. \n\nWhich is exactly why I hate group projects. Even back in grad school, there was always that one person who didn't do anything, and you'd have to scramble at the last minute to fix their part of the presentation. We had this one project on database architecture, and my partner literally copied and pasted an entire Wikipedia article for his section. I had to stay up until 4:00 AM rewriting it so we wouldn't get expelled for plagiarism. I drank so much Red Bull that night my heart was palpitating for two days. I don't touch energy drinks anymore. Actually, I saw this article the other day about how energy drinks are causing a spike in heart issues for people in their twenties. It's crazy what they put in those things. Taurine, guarana... I don't even know what guarana is. It sounds like some kind of tropical lizard, doesn't it? Like an iguana, but... guarana. I used to want a pet lizard when I was a kid, a bearded dragon specifically, but my mom said they smell bad and you have to feed them live crickets. I can't do live bugs. Like I said before with the spiders, I just... I can't. \n\nBut um, yeah, so work has been tough. And outside of work, I've actually skipped my Thursday dance nights the last few weeks. And I mentor this high school robotics team, but I've missed several of those sessions too. I just feel so overwhelmed by the time I get home that I can't bring myself to go out again. So I guess it is interfering, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are repetitive, unwanted, and disturbing to you?\n\nPatient: Um, repetitive and disturbing? No, I don't think so. I don't get, like, scary or weird thoughts popping into my head. It's really just me worrying about making a mistake or forgetting something. \n\nUnless you count getting a really annoying song stuck in your head, which happens to me all the time. Like on Tuesday, the elevator in my apartment building was playing this weird elevator-music version of \"Toxic\" by Britney Spears, and it was just on a loop in my brain for three days. My building's elevator is so incredibly slow, too. It's one of those old ones with the heavy metal doors that feel like they're going to crush you if you don't step through fast enough. My neighbor on the fourth floor—she has this adorable golden retriever named Waffles—she actually got her grocery bag caught in the door once. Waffles was totally fine, he just sat there wagging his tail, but her eggs got smashed everywhere. It took the building superintendent like two whole days to clean it up properly, and the hallway smelled like sulfur the entire time. I tried to submit a complaint to management about it, but they use this awful ticketing system for maintenance requests that looks like it was coded in 1998. As someone in IT support, it physically hurts me to look at their user interface. The submit button doesn't even align with the text boxes, and the dropdown menus glitch if you scroll too fast... \n\nWait, sorry, I'm doing it again. Disturbing thoughts, right? Yeah, no, nothing like that. Just my regular worries.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're checking things to prevent something bad or harmful from happening, like someone getting hurt or something getting contaminated?\n\nPatient: Um, to prevent something bad? Like someone getting hurt? No, I don't... I don't think it's like that. I'm not worried about, you know, a burglar breaking in or my apartment catching on fire or getting some terrible disease. It's not about danger. It's more just... I have to know I did it right. I just have this nagging feeling that I messed up a step or forgot something small. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I'm usually so detail-oriented. It sounds like absolute butter when you type. My boyfriend thinks I'm insane for spending that much time on a keyboard, but he plays golf, which is literally just hitting a little white ball into a hole for four hours, so I don't think he has room to judge. Plus, golf courses are terrible for the environment. All that water they use just to keep the grass perfectly green in the middle of summer? It's so wasteful. I watched this whole documentary about water rights in the Southwest, and it's actually terrifying how low the reservoirs are getting. Lake Mead is basically just a puddle at this point. I tried to bring it up at my family's Thanksgiving dinner, but my uncle Dave—who works in commercial real estate—started getting all defensive about zoning laws, and it turned into this huge argument over the sweet potato casserole. I didn't even get to eat any of the casserole, which is a tragedy because my mom puts those little toasted marshmallows on top...\n\nSorry, I'm rambling again, aren't I? But yeah, to answer your question, no, I'm not checking things to stop something terrible from happening. It's just my own worry about making a mistake.\n\nDoctor: When you feel the need to check something, is it because you're afraid of making a specific mistake, or is it more because you just can't shake the feeling that something might not be right?\n\nPatient: Um, specific mistake versus just a feeling... It's like... my worry used to just be about the condo and whether I left the stove on or locked the door, but now it doesn't stay on the condo anymore. It just jumps around to everything. Like, I'll be worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me because I took too long to text her back. \n\nShe's planning this huge destination wedding in Cabo, and she wants all the bridesmaids to wear these specific seafoam green dresses that cost like four hundred dollars. Which is ridiculous, right? Who spends that much on a dress you're going to wear once? And seafoam green washes me out terribly. I told my mom I'd look like a seasick ghost in the wedding photos, but she just said I need to support my cousin because she's been so stressed about the floral arrangements. Apparently, they wanted white orchids, but there's some kind of shortage, so now they have to use lilies, and my cousin's fiancé is allergic to lilies. Which seems like a pretty big oversight for a wedding planner, honestly. If I were paying someone thousands of dollars to plan my wedding, I'd expect them to know about basic allergies. Yeah, so the worry jumps from my cousin, to whether I'll make a mistake on a support call, to... just bigger things. Like how I'll ever save money, or if I'll ever move forward with my life. Like, I'll review a basic password reset email three times before I hit send, just to make sure I didn't somehow attach the wrong documentation. It's gotten so bad that my coworkers have had to pick up my slack, which makes me feel awful. \n\nOne of my coworkers, Ben, he's been super nice about it, but I know it's annoying. Ben actually just got this really cute rescue dog, a corgi mix, and he brings him into the office sometimes on Fridays. We have this whole Slack channel dedicated to pictures of office pets, and someone posted a video of Ben's dog trying to catch a laser pointer, and it was the funniest thing I've seen all week. Honestly, Slack channels are half the reason I survive the workday. We have one for terrible user quotes, too. Just yesterday, a guy submitted a ticket saying his mouse was broken because it wouldn't work when he lifted it off the desk. Like, he was trying to use it like a TV remote! People are wild. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, it's affecting my personal life too. I've skipped several of my weekly dance nights recently. I usually do salsa on Thursdays, but I've just felt too overwhelmed to even show up. And I missed my last two mentoring sessions with this junior IT girl I'm supposed to be helping. I just feel so drained from all the worrying and checking that I can't bring myself to go out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your checking behaviors and looping thoughts are causing you a lot of emotional pain or distress?\n\nPatient: Um, emotional pain or distress? It makes me feel so tense and miserable, just constantly looping over the same worries and checking things. \n\nWhich is exactly why I tried to get back into knitting last weekend, to give my hands and brain something else to do. I went to this amazing little yarn shop downtown—it's called The Tangled Skein, and it smells like lavender and old wood. I bought this gorgeous, hand-dyed merino wool in this deep, moody teal color. I was going to make a scarf, but then I started worrying that my tension was too tight, so I unraveled the first three rows, like, five times. My cat, Barnaby, thought the yarn was a toy, of course. He's a tuxedo cat, and he has this one white paw that looks like he dipped it in paint. He kept batting at the yarn every time I tried to cast on, and eventually, he just sat right on top of the pattern book. Cats are so funny like that, they always know exactly where you need to look and they just plant themselves right there. Like when I'm trying to read on my Kindle, he'll just shove his face right against the screen. I actually had to buy a special heavy-duty case for it because he knocked it off the nightstand once and chipped the corner. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... the checking and the thoughts. It's really upsetting. It just feels like I'm trapped in this cycle of worrying and fixing and worrying again, and it's just... it's really hard to deal with."
